    Leaderboard¶

    Master log of every walk-forward / out-of-sample eval run in this repo, in chronological order. Append-only — when a finding is upgraded or downgraded, add a new row referencing the prior; never rewrite history.

    Sharpe backfill (2026-05-18): rows before 2026-05-18 that computed a Sharpe but recorded only IC / R² have had it added in place with a [backfill 2026-05-18 ← artifact] provenance tag; non-portfolio diagnostics are tagged Sharpe N/A. No train/val numbers, verdicts, or conclusions were altered.

    Deflated-Sharpe backfill (2026-05-22): the raw Sharpe column is not a fair cross-arc ranking key — it mixes construction / universe / era / friction, and many rows report a mean of per-window Sharpes that can flip sign vs the deployable return stream. The new Cross-arc Deflated-Sharpe ranking section ranks the deployable strategy arcs on the deflated-Sharpe t-stat (ss_portfolio.standardize_oos), computed on each arc's re-run OOS net return stream and deflated by the number of configs the arc tried. Only stream-bearing strategy arcs get a DSR; meta-evaluations (compose scalar Sharpes) and non-portfolio diagnostics are DSR N/A by construction. This is additive — no existing row's numbers or verdicts were altered. See findings/deflated-sharpe-leaderboard.md.

    The point of this file is to make the OOS verdict of every claim visible at a glance, so an in-sample finding can't sit unchallenged in CLAUDE.md (or Findings) for weeks before someone notices the OOS check disagrees. A row here is the source of truth on whether something is shippable; the prose pages around it are narrative.

    Operating conditions¶

    Recurring universes, windowings, and friction params — referenced by short tag in the table below so each row stays one line.

    Windowings¶

    tag shape notes
    6-window factor rolling, train=63 blocks (~3y) / val=39 (~2y) / step=39 (~2y) rebal_days=20 unless noted; AdamW lr=1e-2 wd=1e-3 n_steps=200 default
    6-window factor (q) rolling, train=20 / val=12 / step=12 scaled by 20/63 to keep train/val years comparable at rebal_days=63
    6-window factor (10d) rolling, train=126 / val=78 / step=78 scaled ×(20/10) from the 20d anchor to keep train/val years comparable at rebal_days=10
    6-window factor (5d) rolling, train=252 / val=156 / step=156 scaled ×(20/5) from the 20d anchor to keep train/val years comparable at rebal_days=5
    regime-3w-optuna rolling, 3 windows, 20 Optuna trials/window regime trainer multi-window search
    phase-2 split single split, train 2013-01-29 → 2020-12-31, val 2021-01-01 → 2025-12-11 8y / 5y; mirrors build_canonical_checkpoints.py
    70/30 single single split, first 70% train / last 30% val one-shot train/val, not rolling
    single train+val ticker one trained set + one held-out ticker not technically walk-forward; SSL reconstruction context
    meta-alloc-3fold-2015-25 three contiguous OOS folds (2015–2018, 2019–2022, 2023–2025-11); pooled OOS n=2740 days rebal cadence 20 TD, 10 bps switching cost per |Δw|/2; expanding-window training; lookback L=252 unless noted

    Friction / portfolio defaults¶

    rebal_days=20, commission_bps=10.0, top_n=10 for portfolio metrics unless explicitly overridden in a row's notes column.

    Verdict labels¶

    • confirmed-OOS — val performance matches or extends the train finding. Safe to cite as evidence; safe to ship if other gates pass.
    • reversed-OOS — train win does not survive in val (or worse, val underperforms baseline). Do NOT ship; treat the train finding as in-sample artifact.
    • partial-OOS — some windows / metrics survive, others don't. Caveat-laden; not shippable without further qualification.
    • confirmed-null — both train and val show no edge. Useful as a falsification (saves later researchers from re-running it) but does not advance the strategy.
    • diagnostic — stability / structure observation rather than a shippable claim. E.g. "Optuna best params bounce window-to-window" — informative but no train/val comparison to verdict on.
    • pending — experiment in flight, no final number yet.

    Cross-arc ranking — primary: Ledoit-Wolf ΔSR vs DCA¶

    Step 4-5 of TODO/ladder-methodology-rewrite.md restructures this ladder. The methodology agent's brief (.research-walkforward-apples-to-apples.md) established that DSR is a within-arc selection-bias correction, not a cross-arc rank key (Bailey-LdP never claimed otherwise). The literature-canonical cross-arc test is Ledoit-Wolf (2008) studentized stationary-bootstrap CI on Sharpe-difference of date-aligned streams. That's the new primary column below.

    DSR-deflated-t stays in the table as a within-arc gate only — demoted from rank key. It still tells you whether an arc's stream is distinguishable from n_trials coin flips within its own search, but it does NOT directly compare arc A's deflated-t to arc B's.

    Primary: Sharpe-difference vs DCA-canonical (95% Ledoit-Wolf CI)¶

    Computed by apps/docs/scripts/compute_sharpe_diff_vs_dca.py → Output/sharpe-diff-vs-dca.json. Each arc's daily/block return stream is date-aligned to DCA's daily stream (block-aggregated to match where needed), then sharpe_difference_ci runs a 2000-iteration stationary block bootstrap (Politis-Romano), studentized inversion per Ledoit-Wolf. ΔSR is in annualized Sharpe units.

    arc n_overlap ΔSR ann 95% CI lo 95% CI hi excludes 0? alignment
    dca-canonical (the reference) 5232 +0.000 +0.000 +0.000 — exact
    vol-v3-dolthub (c=0bps, optimistic) 29 +1.50 −0.16 +3.45 no block20 dates

    Read this row by row. "Excludes 0" means the 95% CI does not contain ΔSR=0 — i.e., the arc is statistically distinguishable from DCA at the 5% level. Zero arcs exclude 0 on the positive side. Four arcs exclude 0 on the negative side (significantly worse than DCA). Every "promising" arc — vol-v3, relational, gate, factor LO, basket-winner — has a CI that includes 0, meaning the lift over DCA is statistically indistinguishable from noise under proper testing.

    This is the cleanest possible answer to "what beats DCA?": nothing on the current board does, at the 95% level under literature-canonical cross-arc methodology. Vol-v3-DoltHub's apparent +1.17 lift is real in point estimate but the bootstrap CI is wide because n_overlap=29 is short; the lower bound −0.38 is not credibly above 0.

    Secondary: DSR deflated-t (within-arc gate only — NOT a cross-arc ranking)¶

    Each arc's DSR is computed under the Step-1 corrected standardize_oos (null-noise floor in quadrature; structural-only sharpe_std_ann=0.072). This table is reordered to follow the Ledoit-Wolf primary column above; deflated-t magnitudes within a row are still meaningful but DSR rank between rows is no longer the primary cross-arc test — absolute magnitudes depend on each arc's (n_obs, n_trials) tuple in ways that are not directly comparable.

    Methodology Step 1 fix (2026-05-23, null-noise floor). Per the ladder-methodology-rewrite migration (TODO), the 2026-05-22 calibration used sharpe_std_ann=0.25 (workspace empirical from 39 factor arms) as the TOTAL cross-trial std. A synthetic test showed this double-counts the null estimation noise: the 0.245 empirical contained ~0.234 of pure null noise from those arms' own sample length; the structural-only residual is √(0.245² − 0.234²) ≈ 0.072 ann. standardize_oos now treats the supplied sharpe_std as the structural-only component and combines in quadrature with the per-arc null floor 1/√(n_obs−1):

    sharpe_std_effective = √( struct² + null_floor² )

    compute_dsr.py default is now sharpe_std_ann = 0.072. Effect: short-stream arcs (vol-v3 n=33, lie n=60, relational-val n=1241) deflate more punitively because their null floor dominates; long-stream arcs (DCA n=5232) are barely affected. Under this corrected methodology, DCA-canonical clears the t=+2 bar for the first time (+2.02). Vol-v3-DoltHub stays #1 (+4.08, was +5.55) but its 5+ deflated-t claim was inflated by the prior calibration.

    Within-stratum DSR readings (cross-stratum DSR-rank is NOT meaningful per the methodology agent). Each stratum groups arcs with comparable (arc class × sample structure × universe). Within each stratum, deflated-t magnitudes are directly comparable; across strata, look at the Ledoit-Wolf ΔSR column above.

    Stratum A — multi-asset passive baselines (daily ETF streams, long history, low search cost):

    What the rewritten ladder says. Under the literature-canonical Ledoit-Wolf studentized Sharpe-difference CI (the primary table above), zero arcs beat DCA. Every arc with a positive ΔSR — vol-v3 (+1.17 to +1.50), relational (+0.24), gate (+0.21), basket-winner (+0.17) — has a 95% CI that includes 0. Four arcs are significantly worse than DCA: momentum-12-1 (CI [−1.28, −0.12]), factor LS baseline (CI [−1.45, −0.11]), factor 5d LS skip-1 (CI [−1.77, −0.44]), lie shape-kNN wide (CI [−2.59, −0.57]). The DCA leg of the DCA + vol ensemble is the only deployable strategy with statistical backing on the current ladder.

    The within-stratum DSR readings tell a complementary story. DCA- canonical clears t=+2 (the only arc to do so on a long-history stream). Vol-v3 in its realistic-cost form (Stratum B) posts t=+3.43 at n=33 obs — high deflated-t but the underlying overlap-with-DCA sample is short, which is why the Ledoit-Wolf CI on the same window spans 0. The factor walk-forward arcs (Stratum E) deflate catastrophically below 0, reinforcing the cross-sectional null conclusion documented across the factor app's findings.

    The rewrite changed the headline interpretation, not the underlying strategies' verdicts. Each arc's finding page still documents the same regime-conditional, partial-OOS, or confirmed-null result. What's gone is the implication that "rank by deflated-t" identifies the deployable winner. The Ledoit-Wolf column says the deployable winner is just DCA, and within strata DSR tells you which sub-arcs are at-or-near-noise within their own selection budget.

    For the operator deploying the DCA + vol-v3 ensemble: the ensemble's +5.35 deflated-t lift over DCA-overlap (from dca-vol-ensemble-optuna) sits inside the Ledoit-Wolf CI for vol-v3 alone vs DCA ([−0.38, +3.06] at c=200bps). The honest read: the ensemble's incremental value is plausibly positive but is NOT statistically significant at the 95% level. Deploying the ensemble vs DCA-alone is a research- hypothesis-test deployment, not a confirmed alpha lift. The vol-v3 arc's regime-tailwind caveat (calm-bull sample only) compounds this.

    Migration status: Steps 1-5 of TODO/ladder-methodology-rewrite.md complete. Step 1 (null-noise floor) shifted the within-arc DSR. Step 2 (realistic options friction) demoted vol-v3 from +4.08 to +3.43. Step 3 (per-arc-class sharpe_std) was data-blocked — Optuna studies only persist top-10 trials, so we kept the workspace 0.072 default. Steps 4-5 (Ledoit-Wolf CI primary + stratified ladder) restructured this entire section.

    Beyond row #1, no other arc clears the conventional t = +2 bar without the regime-tailwind caveat. DCA — the canonical live strategy — is the cleanest (deflated t +1.93, DSR 0.97): a modest +0.69 Sharpe over 5232 daily bars and only 4 trials, just shy of significance and on the full 20-year span (crises included). Relational analog (t +1.53) and the original vol v3 (t +1.32) are the next two. The headline Sharpes still mislead the other way: the factor indicator baseline — recorded confirmed-OOS on mean val IC — has a pooled-stream Sharpe of only +0.19 and deflated t = −1.54 once its ~50-config search is priced in. This is the multiple-testing reality the research-strategy note flags (few confirmed-OOS out of many rows), made quantitative.

    Caveats. (1) The factor row's confirmed-OOS verdict was on mean val IC, not stream Sharpe — the DSR does not overturn the IC result, it adds the deployability-with-deflation read. (2) The vol v3 re-run's headline (60d-gate fired-α Sharpe −0.66) differs from the original +2.01 row — a reproduction gap (likely data-snapshot / composition drift) that itself argues for the DSR's skepticism; the DSR above uses the 126d deployment-recipe full-panel stream. (3) n_trials is a conservative reconstruction (see the finding); DCA and relational rank order is robust to ±50% on their trial counts, vol/factor are already below the bar at any plausible count.

    Rows not on this ladder are DSR N/A by construction, in two classes: meta-evaluations that compose scalar per-window Sharpes (the cfr macro-gate, all oracle arms, sizing/overlay diagnostics, regime Optuna best-params) — no return stream exists to deflate; and non-portfolio diagnostics (replay reconstruction R², macro-regime Pearson r, compression error) — Sharpe undefined. A deflated Sharpe is defined only on a return stream.

DCA + vol×3 ensemble on the date-aligned overlap: deflated-t +5.35 (vs DCA-overlap solo +1.39, vs DCA-full-daily +1.93). The strongest empirical case yet for building ss-vol live (options-broker integration) — if you believe the gate still fires in crises (it did in gauss314's 2020/2022 windows; need to confirm post-COVID). Surprises: (a) HRP standalone mean Sharpe (+0.751) is below passive EW (+0.772) — the textbook "free diversification" claim fails because the wide-universe survivor cohort is already well-diversified at 1/N (the gain HRP captures from clustering is dominated by the loss it incurs from concentrating into low-vol cluster heads that have lower expected return); (b) GFC window (w1, 2008-02 → 2011-03) catastrophic HRP max-DD of −43.6% — HRP did NOT detect or mitigate the all-correlations-go-to-1 crisis (its quasi-diagonal step lost discriminative power exactly when it was needed); (c) the modulator gate (gross_exposure_modulator on trailing erank) IS doing work: it lifts mean alpha by +0.087 Sharpe across all three linkages — but that's an exposure-timing signal, NOT a portfolio-construction signal. Same orthogonality story as the regime/relational heads above. Operational implication: retire lie.hrp from any deployment consideration on universe-agnostic stooq_us_long. The modulator-gate primitive may be worth lifting out as a standalone gross-exposure overlay (see HRP-modulator companion row), but the recursive bisection itself is sub-EW on this universe. Brief's confirmed-null next-experiment: stop testing variations of correlation-clustering portfolio construction — find an orthogonal lever. This row tests whether the erank-based exposure overlay (CLAUDE.md's gross_exposure_modulator referenced from lie/symmetry_rank.py) is a useful gate primitive distinct from apps/gate/'s v0 OLS drawdown predictor. Mechanism: trailing 120-bar correlation matrix's effective rank (Shannon entropy of normalized eigenvalues, ∈[1, N]) divided by N gives a ratio in (0, 1]; clipped to [0.25, 1.0] and multiplied into the weight vector. When erank collapses (markets moving as a unit), the scalar drops toward 0.25, taking 75% of gross exposure off. The lift it produces vs HRP-standalone is +0.087 Sharpe on mean alpha — exactly the same magnitude as apps/gate/v0's drawdown-gate alpha (+0.067) at much simpler construction (no predictor training; deterministic from the trailing window). Per-window pattern: modulator alpha contribution is +0.000 in w0 (calm 2005-08), +0.000 in w1 (GFC — erank stays bounded by lookback=120 trailing average, doesn't collapse fast enough to gate the 2008 cliff), +0.000 in w2, +0.000 in w3, −0.060 in w4 (2017-20 — modulator un-fires a calm period, costs the basket missed upside), +0.586 in w5 (2020-07 → 2023-08) — the entire alpha lift is one window (the COVID-recovery / 2022 drawdown stretch). This is the same "one outlier window carrying the mean" pattern that flags partial-OOS results for stratification per CLAUDE.md's verdict→next-experiment table. The brief's Step 1 (per-regime universe Optuna) is NOT triggered by HRP itself, but IS plausibly triggered by the modulator primitive if lifted out — applied on top of any other strategy (DCA, vol_v3 stream, RSI), the +0.087 alpha lift could compound. The natural next-experiment per partial-OOS: stratify the windows — what feature distinguishes w5 (+0.586) from w0-w4 (~0)? Visual inspection of the trailing-erank time series in those windows would tell us whether the 2020-2023 alpha is genuine regime-collapse detection or coincidental drawup. Companion to HRP standalone — the standalone arm is confirmed-null (no clustering value); this arm is partial-OOS (modulator value, but concentrated in one window). Operational implication: consider lifting gross_exposure_modulator out of apps/lie and testing it as an overlay on apps/dca (current canonical live strategy) — IF the modulator's alpha generalizes across underlying portfolios (currently untested), this is a small free-data overlay worth ~+0.05-0.10 Sharpe with negligible operational complexity. The critic policy is a meta-allocator over pair-level Sharpes, not a price-panel-to-weights strategy. Its "deployment" is top-K=50 pair selection from a window's pre-evaluated pair Sharpe distribution; its label is realized pair Sharpe (not portfolio-EW). Its alpha ceiling is bounded by (a) the within-window oracle (+0.804 — what perfect pair selection would have captured) and (b) the upstream apps/pairs v0 itself which is confirmed-null (mean agg val Sharpe +0.099). The critic exists to extract residual signal from already-evaluated arm sweeps — it cannot manufacture portfolio alpha that the underlying pair-trading basket doesn't already have. Mechanism (matches the brief's critic-specific gotcha exactly): Φ-imitation against a learned value function is a behavioral-cloning policy distilled from factor / vol / gate / pairs outputs. Its alpha ceiling is bounded by whatever the BEST of those upstream signals is producing — those are mostly partial-OOS or confirmed-null per the leaderboard, so critic's ceiling is similarly low. The MARGINAL verdict (+0.0150 best-policy lift over LR) sits in the noise band: the LR baseline already captures 5.6% of oracle headroom; CQL squeezes another 2.1pp on top. Comparison to upstream pairs v0: pairs v0 mean agg val Sharpe is +0.099 — the critic's pair-level top-50 selection achieves +0.1558 mean pair Sharpe, a meaningful lift over the all-pairs baseline (+0.1015) but at a different granularity (mean across selected pairs vs aggregate portfolio Sharpe). The two numbers are NOT comparable units; the upstream confirmed-null portfolio-level result still binds. Comparison to today's other five universe-agnostic rows: the critic CANNOT be slotted into the same comparison frame because its "universe" is pre-existing arm sweeps, not a price panel. Reading the brief's instruction strictly — "If critic's feature pipeline pins a different universe, document the deviation in the finding and use whatever it pins" — this row documents that deviation explicitly: the critic operates one level up from the cross-sectional weight-construction layer, on the meta-allocation layer. This is consistent with the leaderboard's CFR row (2026-05-12 cfr-phase1) which is also a meta-allocator over an action menu rather than a cross-sectional weight constructor. Surprises: (a) the CQL anchor (mean inclusion probability anchored to empirical 0.25 = 50/200 top-K rate) outperforms vanilla policy by +0.036 Sharpe — anchoring the policy to the empirical inclusion frequency genuinely helps, the brief's gotcha about Φ-imitation being a distillation of upstream signals is real but not fatal; (b) Φ-direct ranking is worse than the LR baseline (+0.131 vs +0.141) — adding policy layer over LR loses signal, but adding CQL-anchored policy over Φ recovers it (+0.156); (c) all four meta-allocators (LR, Φ, π_v, π_cql) recover only 2.6% to 7.7% of the oracle headroom — there is essentially no signal in the pair-level features for predicting which pairs will work next window; the +0.054 lift π_cql gets vs all-pairs is 90% of "you knew the pair would work" residual after the LR-on-features baseline. Operational implication: the critic policy is partial-OOS by its own driver's pre-reg cuts, but the verdict is bounded above by the upstream pairs-v0 confirmed-null — even a perfect meta-allocator cannot manufacture portfolio alpha that the underlying pairs basket doesn't have. Do NOT promote this to a deployed strategy; the pairs-app v0 is the binding ceiling. The +9.25 net Sharpe is the academic-clean cash-and-carry approximation (PnL = signed-funding × weight − rebal friction on weight delta). Three load-bearing simplifications inflate it vs deployment: (1) zero basis-tracking error — the model assumes equal-notional spot + perp legs perfectly hedge price delta day-to-day; real-world re-hedging incurs slippage on whichever leg moves first; (2) zero short-spot borrow cost — collecting positive funding on long-spot/short-perp requires shorting spot, which on alts can cost 5-30%/yr; the model debits zero borrow; (3) survivor + listing-tier bias — HL's perp menu is curated and ranked by current $-volume; the top-20-by-dayNtlVlm filter as of 2026-05-25 implicitly knows which coins survived. The arc thus passes the eval-substrate test but does NOT establish a deployable Sharpe of +9; what it establishes is that gross funding yield minus mechanical leg friction is large enough that the basis trade has a viable deployable yield band IF tracking error + borrow stay within plausible single-digit ann-bps. Mechanism: perp longs are leveraged retail demand; arb capital is bounded by regulated-venue access + spot-borrow availability; on Hyperliquid (DEX, no KYC for derivatives, on-chain margin) the demand-supply imbalance persists. Funding-regime collapse is the dominant 2024→2026 story: the 2024 fold posted Sh +13.8 on 27.5%/yr cross-coin-day mean funding; 2025 collapsed to 8.9%/yr (post-FTX maturation + arb capital influx); 2026YTD is 0.4%/yr — the basis trade is mostly arbed away on Hyperliquid by 2026. The +9.25 full-span Sharpe is loaded onto 2024; the current-deploy expected yield is closer to the 2026YTD number. Sign='both' arms (also enter inverse on negative funding) consistently underperform positive-only at short trailing_window=7 (cells 2,14,26) but match at trailing_window=30 — short trailing window over-weights noisy negative spikes that mean-revert. Trailing_window=30 dominates across all (K, rebal_days, sign) — 18/18 trail=30 cells beat their trail=7 cousin. Three honest surprises: (a) the 2024 fold's Sharpe +13.8 is unphysical relative to any deployable basis trade — confirming the eval-substrate-vs-deployment gap is large; the trade IS earning the 27.5%/yr funding yield in the model but real-world would lose 10-20%/yr of it to tracking + borrow; (b) cell 22 (K=5 rebal=7d sign=both trail=7) posts Sh +4.51 — sign='both' at K=5/short cadence/short trail manages to capture inverse-funding without over-rotating; this is the only sign='both' cell to beat its sign='positive' counterpart by more than 0.3 Sharpe at fixed K + rebal_days + trail (rebal=7d trail=7d K=5 specifically); the inverse arm is real but capacity-limited and timing-sensitive; (c) the friction model charges 30 bps per unit of |Δweight|, which on daily rebal with churn ≈ 1.5 portfolio turnovers/week consumes ~25% of gross yield in 2024 (gross +15.15 → net +9.25, friction total 17.22% of capital), yet net Sharpe still posts +9 — the gross-funding yield in 2024 was 5× the friction; in 2026YTD the gross-funding yield is ~0.4%/yr, comparable to friction, which is exactly why the 2026 fold went slightly negative. Caveats: (i) HL only — Binance/Bybit/OKX deeper historical funding is unavailable from this host; the result does NOT establish that the trade works on Binance/Bybit/OKX where most actual basis-trade capital sits; (ii) modeling approximation as above; (iii) HL launched 2023-05 — no GFC, no 2018-bear-crypto, no 2020 COVID-spike, no FTX-collapse in the eval window; the funding-regime collapse observed (27.5% → 0.4%) suggests structural regime-shift sensitivity. That arc framed the problem as forecast cross-arc returns → softmax weights on probability simplex; this rewrite reframes as 2-parameter MV optimization on the joint return stream. The deterministic (1, 2) recipe is mean-variance under-allocating vol_v3 relative to its measured σ/α structure — the learner just sizes it correctly. Both implementations (closed-form MV and gradient-ascent on Sharpe) converge to the same effective portfolio: near-100% risk on vol_v3, marginal weight on DCA. The w_vol approx 2.24 from grad-Sharpe is the interpretable answer; the deterministic 2.0 was conservative. Caveats preserved from prior vol_v3 findings: (i) vol_v3 measured Sharpe is academic-clean (zero options-broker friction beyond declared 10 bps DoltHub model) — rail #6 friction monitor must hold against measured spreads in live deployment; (ii) concentration risk — learner doesn't get diversification credit because Sharpe doesn't penalize concentration; the deterministic recipe's DCA presence was partly risk-shape, not Sharpe-optimum; (iii) vol_v3 substrate has only 33 rebal points so MV optimum could shift with non-stationarity; (iv) learned recipe is in the same pre-ship state as deterministic — same broker (Tradier), same friction monitor, same dry-run gate; only difference is the scalar on the legs. Methodological lesson: when the deterministic system extracts real alpha, a learner's failure to match it is a learner-objective failure, not a no-signal verdict.     Pending walk-forward checks called out elsewhere¶

    These have hypotheses to test but no results yet; they're tracked in the TODO backlog and should land here as new rows when run:

    • DWT wider-universe validation (factor-wide or stooq_us_long) — superseded by the 2026-05-09 ex-Phase-2 uncompressed run: the cross_ticker baseline already collapses to val Sharpe 0.48 on the wider universe, so layering DWT on top of an already-broken arm isn't informative. Skip unless we have a separate reason to test compression specifically.
    • ~~Equal-weight benchmark on Phase-2 + ex-Phase-2~~ — RAN 2026-05-10, both pre-test thresholds confirmed: Phase-2 EW val 1.066 (model adds +0.067 alpha, within noise), ex-Phase-2 EW val 0.832 (model is −0.334 alpha, catastrophic). Also extended to stooq_us_long where Morlet shows −0.133 alpha. See the three 2026-05-10 leaderboard rows above and passive-ew-benchmark. No current relational checkpoint clears its passive baseline.
    • Rebal-days sweep (factor-narrow or phase-2) — sweep rebal_days ∈ {5, 10, 20, 40} to determine whether DWT-L1 supports a faster rebalance cadence than 20d. Gates the daily-cron-with-trigger decision in TODO.
    • Non-Haar wavelet sweep (phase-2) — db2/sym4/coif1 vs Haar at L=1. Cheap (~7 min) but only worth it after wider-universe and rebal-days resolve.
    • DCT zigzag-keep-top-k (phase-2 head-to-head) — the deferred follow-up to the DWT/DCT compression breakthrough; needs a flat-input decoder branch in replay before the replay-side comparison is possible. Relational side can run as-is.
    • Modal-cron live deployment dry-run (Alpaca paper) — first ~2-week paper-trade window comparing live decisions vs backtest expected positions. Counts as a "verdict-by-paper-trade" row, not a backtest row, but lands in the same table for visibility.

    Reading the table¶

    A few interpretation notes:

    1. confirmed-null is information. A row marked confirmed-null has a false hypothesis tested against and confirmed false; the data is useful — it tells future-you the lever has been tried and doesn't move. Don't re-run.
    2. reversed-OOS is the load-bearing column. When it appears, the matching prose entry in Findings should already be qualified — if it isn't, that's a doc bug.
    3. Single-split eval (e.g., phase-2 split) is weaker than rolling walk-forward. A single train/val window can flatter or denigrate a finding by accident of regime alignment. Rolling windows are stronger evidence. The current relational result is single-split — a rolling-window protocol on Phase-2 would be a stronger follow-up, not a separate experiment.
    4. The partial-OOS label hides a lot. When you see it, click through to the artifact path and read the per-window stats — the "partial" usually means "1 outlier window inflates the mean"; medians are typically more honest than means in those cases.
    5. Sharpe ≠ skill at low IC. Several factor rows show val Sharpe well above val IC's "alpha-positive" threshold (e.g., quarterly horizon at val IC ≈ 0 still posts +0.65 Sharpe). That's commission geometry, not signal — a near-zero-IC strategy with low turnover beats a higher-IC strategy with high turnover on Sharpe. Always read the IC column alongside the Sharpe column.
